# Development log

This record starts with the 2026-10-07 Rust source audit and initial Swift port. Detailed entries run newest first; the index below reads from the beginning. The milestone order follows recorded dates and dependencies, not an invented per-edit timestamp sequence. Current implementation facts are in [STATUS](STATUS.md); older entries describe their own checkpoint.

The 2026-10-08 documentation audit added stable navigation and a clearly labeled retrospective expansion of the initial work. Earlier generic logs such as `artifacts/full-tests.log`, `artifacts/verification.json` and `artifacts/photato-release-verification.json` are **rolling summaries**: their present contents do not prove an older entry's counts. The [evidence inventory](DEVELOPMENT_HISTORY.json) hashes surviving files and records their actual reported outcomes, including failures. Missing historical detail is not reconstructed by guesswork.

Follow [the development-record requirements](../CONTRIBUTING.md#development-record) for every new investigation, implementation, test and correction. Preserve original evidence before rerunning fixed-name tools. New notes must distinguish what was done, how it was checked and what remains unverified.

## 2026-10-19 — Direct linear HDR editing and composite qualification

1. Classify step114 as verified progress. Revalidate all 393 Swift source/test hashes, universal ZIP and Xcode project against checkpoint114. Preserve the complete baseline under `artifacts/runs/20261019T212322Z-115-linear-editing`; prior validation and isolated UI processes are terminal. Continue major D01 by independently analyzing the measured RGB32 merged delta and migrating the affected linear editing paths. Preserve full Photoshop/API/platform requirements; no major function is closed by this starting audit.

2. Inspect existing LayerMerge, its independent Photoshop fixtures, desktop routing and both API adapters. The layer merge transaction still round-trips RGB32 through transfer functions; its workflow tests currently allow RGB error. Replace that tolerance with exact linear RGBA byte comparison before changing the engine, to capture the deficiency. Existing API has 17 commands and no merge operation; actionJSON supports only component select/fill.

3. Preserve baseline01 compile failure (missing try on rgbaData), repair test syntax, then baseline02 reproduces six RGB32 merge/export exact-byte failures. Independent ICC/resource inspection finds all three 572-byte profiles identical (SHA256 42d1e258eddfda5492ad878a0077e1365c9662cfe90789c9663333f24bf3c3ff); a differing embedded profile does not explain the existing merged delta. Retain per-channel opaque-base sample comparisons in icc-and-base-delta01.json. Migrate LayerMerge to direct linear input/intermediate/output and remove final transfer; keep integer arithmetic unchanged.

<a id="dev-step-114"></a>

## 2026-10-19 — Exact RGB32 sample preservation

1. Classify step113 as progress: source-format Save/Save As, optional API saving and content-aware file association are implemented and qualified. Verify all 391 Swift hashes, ZIP and Xcode project against checkpoint113; preserve the baseline under `artifacts/runs/20261019T204913Z-114-rgb32-preservation`. Prior validation sessions are terminal. Inspect Surface, native tile/archive serialization, PSD channel/profile import/export and full/group rendering. Guessed Pixel.swift, NativeArchive.swift, RegionalRenderer.swift and PSD_DEPTH_REFERENCES.json paths are absent; rg locates Color.swift, Document.swift and GroupRenderer.swift. No pending process is restarted.

2. Recheck Adobe's PSD format specification and current HDR support documentation. Trace the recorded 96-byte RGB32 round-trip difference to the linear→encoded Float32→linear conversion at PSD boundaries. A more accurate transfer function alone cannot retain all source float bits after a many-to-one rounded conversion. Plan explicit linear RGB storage in Surface, preserving the existing encoded pixel-view API while adding exact linear access. Native archives must declare/version that representation, crop/expansion/history must preserve it, and shared PSD/API paths must use it. This is a storage foundation for complete linear editing, not a cache of original file bytes. Existing wide-gamut/profile, unsupported HDR operations and full Photoshop rendering requirements remain open.

3. baseline01 reproduces the RGB32 failure: one test, one assertion, 96 differing working-composite bytes; the RGB16/masked references remain exact. Implement explicit linearRGB Surface storage, encoded-view and exact linear access, unchanged-component retention, storage-aware crop/expansion/serialization, raster packing version2 and native archive version9 with old versions still readable. PSD RGB32 imports retain linear samples; export reads those exact samples. ImageIO profile conversion accepts the declared storage representation. focused01-storage passes29 tests in1.550 seconds, including zero differing bytes for all three metadata fixtures and legacy tile/native migration coverage. This removes the measured import/export drift, but exact merged output and editing paths still need independent qualification.

4. focused02 fails compiling a new history test because EditorSession is a value type declared with let. Preserve its log and use var. focused03 executes five tests: core storage/history/archive tests and three-cycle original-Photoshop layer preservation pass, but three compression variants fail the independent literal merged-plane assertion. The old compositor loses signed zero and ordinary low bits and converts the largest finite HDR sample to black through an overflowing transfer round trip. Preserve failed03 source hashes and repair direct linear composition plus merged output; no whole-feature closure is claimed.

5. Direct linear raster composition, ordinary group/clipping composition and merged PSD encoding now avoid an encoded intermediate. Opaque Normal identity preserves exact sample bits including negative zero and the largest finite HDR sample. focused04-linear-render passes118 selected tests in10.146 seconds, including literal planes across all three layer compression modes and existing group references. Add grouped/regional and real HTTP RGB32 upload/rename/history/download plus both action-job interfaces. Publish the storage representation, version and explicit precision limits in optional API capabilities. Normal source-save review stays in place while profile, transformed/adjusted/fractional HDR parity remains open.

6. focused05 stops at a test compile error: Layer.isClipped is read-only; the stored flag is clipped. Repair the test fixture and refresh the metadata test comment. Run swift-format on changed source/tests, save the393-file freeze, then rerun the new storage/API suite. The old verify-app-package.py is pinned to step058 counts and is unsuitable for current package qualification; use a new run-specific verifier.

7. focused06 executes22 tests; the new job test fails because the existing resource decoder rejects every non-RGB8 input before action dispatch. Native upload/rename/download and all linear tests pass. Extend both shared job interfaces to RGB16/32 PSD composite-RGB selection/preservation, keep component selection/writes restricted toRGB8 in the common interpreter, retain RGB8 PNG input, and reject RGB32 PNG output explicitly. This is a narrow preservation path, not general HDR action support. Add matching capability/docs and high-depth action rejection tests; preserve all failed logs.

8. focused07 passes36 storage/imaging/HTTP/action tests with zero failures in4.914 seconds. Both job APIs now match desktop PSD bytes; original Photoshop RGB32 layer samples survive all tested routes. Add explicit RGB32 PNG-output and component-Fill rejection assertions before full/optimized/universal qualification. Freeze393 Swift source/test files as tested-source-hashes03.json. Preserve rolling artifacts before qualification.

9. The full01 run exposes full-versus-incremental render mismatches in ColorRegionRenderTests and FocusRenderTests. Trace these to RenderSnapshot copying a linear patch through pixel()/setPixel(), which rounds changed linear samples. Let the running suite finish to capture all failures before editing. The earlier nonexistent BlendMode.swift/Blend.swift and AdobeActionPipeline.swift guesses are resolved by rg to Color.swift and AdobeActionAssets.swift; no tool failure is treated as source evidence.

10. full01 terminates normally with1040 tests and three assertions failing in the two incremental-render tests; no tests skipped. Repair RenderSnapshot patch copying to use linearPixel/setLinearPixel with row cancellation checks for linear surfaces. Extend the literal storage regression to compare exact full and incremental raw-linear bytes including extreme/signed-zero samples. Preserve freeze03 and full01, create freeze04 and rerun full/optimized/build validation under new names.

11. full02 passes1040 tests with zero failures/skips after the incremental copy repair. Optimized qualification and universal build run sequentially on the same frozen sources. Add a bounded independent Python PSD32 plane inspector for the two-layer original reference: it reads raw/RLE/ZIP/predicted payloads without Swift or image color conversion, identifying122880 RGBA layer bytes and73728 original merged RGB bytes. Self-inspection is only parser setup; exact output preservation still requires comparing the packaged app’s actual saved file.

12. Optimized release02 passes628 tests with zero failures/skips in16.502 seconds. Universal packaging runs next. Existing RasterDecodeBudget Sendable warnings also appear in the accepted step113 optimized log; no new warning suppression is added. During independent Photoshop reference setup, refresh CUA after an inactive binding, recover a clipboard timeout with the visible path field, then observe a selected disposable RGB32 source with Open disabled. Re-selection, explicit Photoshop filter and bounded coordinate attempts do not open it. Preserve AX/screenshot, restore All Readable Documents and cancel; original Untitled-1 remains untouched. This is a chooser observation, not proof that Photoshop rejected PSD content.

13. Universal build02 succeeds; run-specific verification binds393 source/test files,1040 full/628 optimized tests,arm64+x86_64 and macOS14 metadata, unchanged Xcode project/menu inventory/licenses/icon, and a clean strict-signed ZIP. As before, the synced Desktop copy later acquires Finder/resource-fork metadata and fails strict verification; do not advertise that copy as signature-clean. Launch an isolated identity from the verified Release ZIP. A Go-to-folder highlight again leaves Open disabled until focusing the file list and typing the filename’s first letter commits selection. Open RGB32, rename Precision to HDR preserved, Undo/Redo, save native version9, close/reopen it, then Save As PSD. Capture AX and actual screenshot. Photoshop opens that saved RGB32 PSD with observed layer dimensions48×32 and offset13,9; close the disposable output without saving, retaining the user’s original Untitled-1.

14. Independent Python inspection of the actual live saved PSD confirms all122880 original layer RGBA bytes unchanged, including the renamed layer. The original73728-byte merged RGB reference still differs in13569 bytes /12986 Float32 samples, maximum absolute sample error4.470348358154297e-08; exported alpha is fully opaque. This is a measured remaining compositor gap, not full Photoshop equivalence. Preserve the native/PSD files and both comparison reports. Quit the isolated app; capture its automatic workspace panelDock migration, verify only that migration differs, and restore the exact original workspace bytes after confirming the app exited. Source probe and original Photoshop document remain unchanged.

15. Review the original merged cache outside the upper layer before attributing its differences to arithmetic. Create a separate copy of the live PSD, open it in Photoshop, change top-layer opacity from 100% to 99% and back to 100%, then Save and verify saving completes. Independent parsing of that freshly recalculated output confirms all layer bytes remain exact and reproduces the same 13,569-byte / 12,986-sample merged RGB difference, maximum 4.470348358154297e-08. Preserve both the initial cache review and recalculated evidence; close the disposable document. The remaining discrepancy needs color-management/compositor diagnosis, and is not waived by small visual error.

16. Finalize storage/API/reference manifests, command coverage, status/plan and active checkpoint114. All 393 Swift source/test hashes remain at freeze04; the universal package, Xcode project and earlier checkpoint113 remain bound and unchanged after qualification. The first development-history refresh/check passes: 114 milestones, 26,564 evidence files, 16,026 logs, zero missing references. Record that result in the active checkpoint and run the final refresh/check after this note. The goal remains active, major D01 remains open, and optional minor/detail work has not started.

## 2026-10-19 — Source-format Save workflow

1. Classify the preceding goal turn as progress: metadata preservation, TIFF encoding repair, native-extension integration and verified release/checkpoint112 changed authoritative state. Verify all387 source/test hashes, current ZIP and Xcode project against checkpoint112, then preserve the baseline under `artifacts/runs/20261019T200618Z-113-source-save-workflow`. Previous validation sessions are terminal, so no process is restarted. Read current instructions/checkpoint, document controller, shared codecs, persistence tests, optional API state/permissions and AppKit NSDocument saving headers. A guessed Raster.swift path is absent; rg --files locates Surface.swift. No source changes yet.

2. Adobe’s current Save guide distinguishes saving the current format/association from Save a Copy, and exposes format-dependent layers/profile options. Plan format-aware Save/Save As through NSDocument’s coordinated writing and change tracking, shared background encoders and a separate default-off API local-write permission. Save formats must preserve supported editable structure; flattened-only cases remain Save a Copy. Native recovery continues as a separate .photato archive. Existing source-profile conversion and RGB32 exact-byte gaps remain explicit major requirements rather than being represented as resolved by ownership/format controls. Do not add arbitrary filesystem paths to remote requests; API Save As needs an explicitly chosen local destination boundary.

3. Implement DocumentSavePolicy/DocumentSaving, format-aware NSDocument open/read/write/ownership, native Save As format controls and explicit conversion limits, and native recovery type. Add document.save/document.saveAs through the shared save operation, separate session-local save permission, native-selected Save As folder, filename/overwrite/symlink/external-change guards and capability/state schemas. Keep both Adobe resource routes unchanged over shared encoders. Split document type registration into editable supported formats and import-only types. The guessed AutomationGuard.swift file is absent; guards live in AutomationService.swift.

4. focused01 compiles but exposes four identity assertions: the old import-only PNG expectation must change, and canonical alias lookup no longer matches the newly associated source URL. It then deadlocks: sample83021 shows the new asynchronous save override waiting on AppKit’s document-file serialization semaphore during an autosave callback. Preserve source/log/sample, deliberately terminate only that confirmed test process, and retain terminal exit1 without claiming a completed test run. Keep recovery callbacks synchronous in AppKit’s original activity context; regular explicit saves continue to pre-encode. Extend canonical identity lookup to associated and import-only URLs, update the PNG lifecycle test to use real Save As, and update the independent API registry expectation from15 to17. Add scrolling to the expanded native API window so its new permission controls remain reachable.

5. focused02 passes23 existing lifecycle tests in3.026 seconds with zero failures/skips, confirming the autosave/alias repair. Add eight source-save/API regressions covering every writable format, actual NSDocument ownership/dirty/history, representation rejection, PSD recovery archive, stale/cancelled/failed encodes, duplicate destination ownership, HTTP permissions/equivalence and filename/replace/symlink/external-change/revocation. focused03 fails compiling the new tests because the existing edit helper returns an HTTP status Int, not a response tuple. Preserve the test candidate/log; use the actual helper return and a separate HTTP GET for returned document state. No tests execute in focused03.

6. focused04 passes45 tests in3.857 seconds, zero failures/skips. Review the already-known color/float fidelity gap before permitting source-file replacement: imported non-sRGB profiles and RGB32 PSDs need explicit Save As format review; ordinary Save must not silently replace those originals while exact preservation remains unresolved. Add a sourceSaveNeedsReview state/guard and regression preserving the original bytes; explicit Save As retains its visible conversion notice and resets this initial source review state. This guard is temporary incomplete fidelity coverage, not a substitute for implementing profile/float preservation or closing D01.

7. focused05 passes46 tests in4.090 seconds, zero failures/skips. Freeze391 files and prepare an isolated Debug app with updated type registration. Live Open initially shows a highlighted path with disabled Open; keyboard Home/Down selects the file and enables it. Open the disposable original Photoshop RGB8 masked fixture, change top-layer opacity60→75 and Command-S: it writes back to PSD, retains the association and clears the dirty tab. Shift-Command-S exposes a real missing-format-control defect: only the explanatory accessory is visible. Preserve AX/screenshot/source, cancel, close the saved probe and quit the isolated app. Add an explicit native format popup using the shared copy accessory with Save-specific notices/eligible types, and expose its selected type through NSDocument’s fileTypeFromLastRunSavePanel. Add a regression for actual accessory, extension/content type and selected type; live end-to-end confirmation remains required.


8. focused06 passes47 tests in4.059 seconds, zero failures/skips, and the391-file freeze02 records the format-control repair. The isolated Debug2 app opens the disposable Photoshop mask fixture; native Command-S saves opacity75 to its PSD and clears the dirty tab. Save As now offers Photato/Photoshop for this layered model, changes the extension and notice when Photato is selected, writes a binary native archive and adopts its URL. Close/reopen retains two layers, opacity75, density50.2 and feather1.5. The API window exposes17 commands and separate default-off local-save permission/folder selection with scrolling. Preserve AX/screenshots and output hashes. Quit the validation app; inventory confirms no ui113 app running and workspace bytes match the prelaunch copy. Some CUA keystrokes were refused after focus changed; refreshing the actual state and retrying completed the intended operation without altering other documents.

9. Open only the disposable Photato-saved PSD in Photoshop2026. It accepts the file, displays both original layer names, Multiply, opacity75%, mask density50% and feather1.5px. Capture AX/screenshots, close the probe without saving, and verify the original user Untitled-1 remains. This qualifies the observed file/structure properties; it is not an independent byte-equality comparison. Start unique full01, release-focused01 and build01 validation sequentially on the frozen sources; full validation/package qualification remain pending at this note.

10. full01 completes1031 tests with three failed assertions in two older tests: PathWorkflowTests still expects only the native writable type, and WorkflowTests still expects PNG to lack a file association. The remaining1029 tests pass. Preserve the run and both original tests. Update the bundle/type check to compare all Editor registrations while still asserting the canonical native type, and replace the old PNG assumption with actual source association plus a failed save-back after adding an editable layer, verifying original bytes and dirty state. Application source is unchanged from the live Debug2 check. Freeze03 differs only in these two test files. A preparation assertion incorrectly depended on filesystem enumeration order and stopped before running tests; compare the changed-path set, then run full02, expanded optimized tests and build02 sequentially.

11. full02 passes1031 tests; release-focused02 passes532 tests with zero failures/skips. While build02 runs, final source review identifies a missed save-back case: Format(fileExtension:) recognizes jpeg/tiff but not common jpg/tif aliases, and associated format is inferred from the filename rather than decoded content. A mislabeled opaque PNG could consequently be saved as lossy JPEG without an explicit format choice. Preserve the pre-repair source and let the existing build finish before changing compiled files. Record unsupported edge cases and keep full functionality/pixel/shortcut/preferences gates open.

**References:** [Adobe feature catalogue](https://www.adobe.com/products/photoshop/features/all.html), [Adobe alignment](https://helpx.adobe.com/in/photoshop/desktop/create-manage-layers/create-layer-compositions/align-content-of-layers-and-groups.html), [Adobe distribution](https://helpx.adobe.com/ca/photoshop/desktop/create-manage-layers/create-layer-compositions/distribute-layers-groups-evenly.html), accessed 2026-10-08. Adobe describes six edge/center alignments and six edge/center distributions plus two spacing operations. These docs alone do not qualify rounding, masks or enablement; measure the installed app.


1. Generated three original 240×180 RGB8 PSD inputs (four unequal rectangles, one layer, overlapping rectangles), with four transparent pixels of storage padding on every side. Reproduced all three byte-identically; added a separate two-layer enablement probe without replacing the original generator. Input/PSD/reference hashes and original-data provenance are retained. No installed Adobe content or user document is used.

Group/mask/type/shape/hidden/lock/depth semantics are not qualified.

## 2026-10-08 — Classic native arithmetic candidate

1. Revalidated all 242 Swift source/test hashes and exported independently saved controls/opaque rows for fifteen references. A macOS14-targeted optimized Swift sweep tests 3,072 precision/scale/operation-order combinations. Integer-scaled stop values with Double midpoint mapping and weighted-sum linear interpolation reproduce the previously unresolved linear ramp exactly. Unlike the prior unscaled candidate, the arithmetic keeps the signed-fraction numerator exact until final conversion. No epsilon or per-pixel correction is used; Int128 is unnecessary.
2. Compiled a complete isolated Swift candidate (uniform cubic, segment bounds and output conversion). All fifteen existing 4,097-position reference rows match with zero working-level error. These are candidate results, not production test passes. ## 2026-10-08 — Classic gradient lookup investigation

**Scope:** the previous turn made verified progress with RGB16 tone mapping. **Evidence:** `artifacts/runs/20261008T211519Z-051-classic-gradient-lookup/record.json`. Inspect saved 4,097-position gradient rows to distinguish coarse lookup construction from continuous interpolation. The identity output's one-working-level deficit except every sixteenth index suggests an intermediate lookup/rounding stage; this is a hypothesis, not an implementation rule.

1. Ran independent Python candidate sweeps against the Photoshop-saved 65-color Classic ramp. Coarse lookup intervals of 2–64 positions did not improve on continuous interpolation; reject that simple table hypothesis. Working-grid input reconstruction and fixed fractions still leave a 380-working-level maximum error. Inspecting worst samples identifies cubic overshoot beyond the two segment colors. Clamping each output channel to those two endpoints reduces the maximum to **one working level** (1,890 of 4,097 pixels still differ in the best candidate). This is empirical diagnostic evidence; production remains unchanged pending independent cases and rounding qualification. Preserved candidate scripts/reports and the first segment analyzer before adding the bounded-output candidate.

2. Generated eight original RGB16 Classic probes: ascending/descending linear identity, smoothed identity, 65-color linear palette, offset endpoints, constant color, uneven three stops and a changed midpoint. All inputs regenerated into a new directory. The independent inspector now compares controls/pixels and multiple rounded/bounded candidates; no production source has changed.

3. All eight acquired controls and reproduced inputs validate independently. Fitted slopes of the uneven three-stop reference identify uniform-parameter cubic coefficients: neighboring color differences divided by two, without native stop-distance scaling. Integer midpoint placement reduces the changed-midpoint error to one working level. A final-output bucket conversion reproduces neutral and 65-color ramps, but arbitrary stops still differ. Solving every linear-output bin for feasible endpoint values then identifies a signed fractional stop expansion. Combining these rules matches all **36,873 opaque-row RGB pixels** across nine ramps (eight new plus the prior 65-color smooth palette), with zero working-level differences. This is an independent Python candidate, not production Swift output. Generated six further deterministic holdouts with arbitrary colors/positions, partial smoothness, changed midpoints and signed-fraction tie boundaries before considering implementation.

All probes are closed and the original Photoshop document is unchanged. The first candidate fails tie colors and odd-span/30% midpoints. Revised nearest-midpoint rounding and downward color ties eliminate those errors; five holdouts then match, while the random linear palette retains seven one-working-level differences. The failed and repaired candidate reports/scripts remain separate.
5. Exact rational arithmetic validates full RGBA output, including the half-alpha fourth row, for fourteen of the fifteen reference ramps. The random linear palette differs at three positions (12 pixels across four rows), up to two stored levels; mathematical exactness alone does not reproduce Photoshop’s floating rounding. An isolated Swift typecheck rejects Int128 at the macOS14 target because it requires macOS15; retained the failure and rejected raising the deployment requirement. Compare equivalent Double operation orders without epsilon adjustments before choosing a native implementation.


6. Tested 450 then 900 equivalent Double-operation configurations, including interpolation split at the midpoint; the best still differs at five opaque pixels. ## 2026-10-08 — persistent native gradient preset library

Photato now has a shared native gradient library with searchable nested folders, multi-selection, rename, duplicate, delete, drag reordering and explicit Move To destinations. Window → Gradients, the Gradient tool and the fill editor use the same library. Properties and the fill editor expose Save Preset. Capturing a preset keeps its exact native color/opacity controls, midpoints, smoothness and interpolation, without changing document history or committing an in-progress fill draft. Clicking a preset recolors an existing Gradient Fill with any tool; double-click, the context command or a canvas drop creates a retained fill in one undo step. New fills capture foreground/background colors and use a mask when a selection exists. That last behavior is not yet qualified against Photoshop preset activation.

The bounded binary native archive stores stable IDs, flat validated folder records, selection/current preset, full-precision Swift definitions, GRD descriptors and original imported files. GRD alone quantizes position/midpoint controls, so native definitions are retained separately. Atomic background edits publish only after successful file replacement. Malformed imports, failed writes, cancellation, overlapping operations and cyclic moves preserve the previous committed state. Corrupt saved data is preserved rather than overwritten. Existing documents own their fill definitions and survive preset deletion.

Selected/all GRD export preserves unavailable noise/CMYK definitions and unknown descriptor fields. Renaming an unavailable record changes its name without enabling substitute rendering. Exporting an unchanged original imported root retains the whole original file byte for byte, including through a native save/reload. Duplicated groups preserve records but do not copy whole-file root metadata. Empty groups survive native persistence; an empty GRD export remains unavailable. Imports and native archives are bounded at 16 MiB and 64 MiB respectively, with count/depth validation.

The bounded native PAT reader supports RGB, grayscale, indexed and packed one-bit planes; 8/16/32-bit samples; raw and PackBits compression; channel rectangles and transparency. ## 2026-10-08 — automatic Spot Healing and native patch completion

Ported all three PhotoCraft inpainting paths into native Swift: multi-scale Wexler/PatchMatch completion with similarity-weighted votes (Content-Aware), overlap-feathered block synthesis (Create Texture), and ring-error proximity matching with a content-aware fallback. SplitMix64 seeds, candidate order, Float32 operation order and fixed 16-row propagation bands match the Rust numerical functions. PatchMatch/voting currently run sequentially; cancellation is checked through the pyramid, row bands, iterations, sampling and commit. The pipeline shares the previous membrane solver and round-tip coverage/painting infrastructure. There is no model download, external service or Rust runtime dependency.

Spot Healing operates in document coordinates regardless of the target's stored pixel frame. It synthesizes a domain two pixels beyond the stroke, fits gradients over a one-pixel expansion and known boundary ring, then applies coverage to the target. Current-layer and Sample All Layers contexts are captured before the stroke. The latter supports empty cropped retouch layers without modifying the original image. Mask sampling remains scalar independently of stored alpha. Existing off-canvas storage/mask positions survive target expansion. Entirely transparent image contexts return an exact no-op before synthesis. A search region with no surrounding pixels reports an error rather than inventing a black repair. Search regions are bounded to 4,194,304 pixels; public algorithm buffers require finite magnitudes at most 1e12 and one to four channels. These are documented implementation limits, not Photoshop equivalence claims.

## 2026-10-08 — sampled Healing Brush in native Swift

Ported PhotoCraft's cascadic membrane/SOR solver and seamless-clone equations from `crates/algo/src/poisson.rs`. The native implementation validates dimensions, lengths and finite inputs, bounds individual repair regions to 4,194,304 pixels, checks cancellation and rejects numeric overflow without committing a partial result. Channels are solved independently in encoded sample space; existing RGB/32 resampling and final compositing retain the native linear-light behavior. Healed integer channels clamp to the unit interval; float RGB retains positive extended values. No Rust code is linked into Photato.

Clone Stamp and Healing Brush now share round-tip coverage, blending and atomic layer-storage expansion. Healing samples an immutable source and destination boundary, preserving sampled texture while correcting the colour mismatch. Current & Below / All Layers capture the destination document's visible context, so cross-document sampling can repair onto an empty cropped retouch layer. Pixel masks retain their outside scalar and geometry. Selections clip final paint; the solver's boundary is not selection-restricted, a remaining Photoshop qualification gap. Coverage lookups now reject out-of-grid coordinates before computing tile keys, preventing a right-edge sample from aliasing the next tile row. Existing Clone Stamp regressions pass.

The J group's Healing Brush is connected to Option-click sampling, the five shared Clone Source slots, aligned/restarted sources, transforms, overlays, pressure/spacing/hardness/opacity/flow, sampled layer policies and Ignore Adjustments. Clone and Healing settings are independent. Background preview uses the same immutable command as commit; Escape or tool switching cancels, and successful strokes add one undo step. The native options show seven implemented healing blend modes. Pattern, Replace and Diffusion are not implemented and remain in the in-app gap map and reference manifest. The workflow does not claim Spot Healing or generative removal.

All 15 Healing tests also pass under release optimization in **0.23 seconds**. The independent generator compiles the original Rust numerical functions unchanged, replacing only Rayon scheduling/interleaving with a sequential harness. **68,836/68,836 Float32 results across nine regions are bit-identical on this Apple Silicon host in debug and release**; tests allow a 2e-6 cross-compiler tolerance. Clone Stamp now grows cropped raster and pixel-mask storage in the existing sample grid. Newly allocated raster areas are transparent; mask padding retains the mask's exact implicit outside scalar. Original source/Rust tip vectors remain unchanged. No archive version change is required. The three StoreKit integration tests remain historical and unchanged.

## 2026-10-08 — Hue/Saturation range transfer correction

Verified that the public app, menus, About window and universal executable are named **Photato**, and that the packaged icon derives from the supplied `../PhotatoIcon.png` without altering the source artwork. The active Photoshop-parity work continues under that name.

The four pinned Photoshop adjustment references exposed a basic mismatch in the original Rust equations: Master lightness shifts toward black/white, while color-range lightness contracts toward the pixel's minimum/maximum channel. Master lightness now runs before hue/saturation; range contraction uses an algebraic RGB form without unstable HSV divisions. Range weights no longer fade artificially with low chroma. Grays, hidden RGB, excluded colors and true identity edits keep their exact samples. Range lightness clamps in stored order, and pure lightness edits avoid unnecessary HSL reconstruction.

Master and range saturation now apply sequentially. A native Swift adaptation of psd-tools' empirical 21×21 overlap grid and nonlinear positive-range contribution replaces additive saturation. The MIT notice is retained in `LICENSE-psd-tools`, the app's resources and Licenses & Credits. The table is prepared into the hue LUT outside the pixel loop; no Python, SciPy or Rust runtime is introduced. The positive boost remains an approximation: a reciprocal response capped at 100× avoids a singularity at +100 and improves the references without biasing every positive setting by 0.01. Neutral grid axes preserve exact no-effect behavior. This is measured progress, not a claim of the proprietary Photoshop algorithm.

| Saved Photoshop reference | Maximum encoded channel error, before → after | Mean per-pixel max error, before → after | Differing RGB/8 pixels after |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lightness | 0.94901961 → 0.01749779 | 0.15768446 → 0.00215699 | 19,881 / 40,000 |
| Combined | 0.73731190 → 0.26558697 | 0.08230844 → 0.00665064 | 29,746 / 40,000 |
| Saturation | 0.43629897 → 0.09424768 | 0.04661153 → 0.00287314 | 22,220 / 40,000 |
| Colorize (unchanged) | 0.01142710 → 0.01142710 | 0.00372146 → 0.00372146 | 32,282 / 40,000 |

All four exactness gates remain **false**. The combined reference has a substantial isolated residual; near-gray/high-saturation behavior, overlap interpolation, integer quantization and underlying composition remain open. Reference ceilings were tightened to the new measured behavior, with mean-error checks for all four files. Before and after reports are `artifacts/hue-reference-before-range-correction.json` and `artifacts/hue-reference-corrected.json`. Temporary transfer prototypes compared processing order, chroma weighting, reciprocal boost, nonlinear contribution and overlap; their logged measurements are diagnostics, not whole-document acceptance results.

Four additional core tests cover distinct Master/range lightness endpoints, near-gray range hue/lightness, lightness-before-saturation, finite extremes and ordered overlap clipping. All 23 Hue/Saturation tests pass; the full package suite passes **283 tests**, zero failures/skips. The three unchanged StoreKit integration tests remain historical. An initial test run caught an unintended extended-HSL saturation clamp change; the original hue-only behavior was restored and the passing full suite verifies it. Historical Rust vectors remain intact: cases 0, 1, 2 and 8 retain 2,980 RGB and 2,980 encoded-float samples, while contradicted cases are explicitly superseded by analytical and Photoshop-composite evidence. `docs/HUE_SATURATION_ORACLE.json` pins the reference source, hash, license and limits.

Native archives retain their version and parameters. Existing adjustment layers intentionally adopt the corrected rendering when reopened. Live controls and Photoshop invocation remain unverified while the desktop is locked; the saved references support continued headless work. The full Photoshop feature, pixel, shortcut, preference and platform gates remain open.

## 2026-10-07 — real Hue/Saturation PSD references and Colorize correction

Fetched and pinned four unmodified MIT-licensed psd-tools adjustment files at commit `1b8c1d404d1b976748e7dbbca33ed5898663371b`. Import exposed large differences between the original source equations and saved Photoshop composites. Colorize changed RGB lightness after creating the tint; applying lightness to HSL before RGB synthesis corrects washed-out highlights/shadows. Across the 40,000-pixel Colorize reference, maximum encoded error falls from **0.47039384** to **0.01142710** (97.6% reduction), and mean per-pixel maximum error from **0.04734646** to **0.00372146**. **32,282 pixels still differ at document depth**, so byte identity is not achieved. Its float expectations are measured in linear light: near-black encoded residuals magnify input transfer rounding by 12.92. The old Rust Colorize/nonzero-lightness cases 9 and 10 remain in the fixture as historical evidence, but no longer define correct behavior; 7,450 RGB and 7,450 encoded-float source samples remain directly compared. Existing workflows also test bounded 136-byte AHU loading. ## 2026-10-07 — Hue/Saturation, color ranges and Desaturate

Added native Master and six-range Hue/Saturation, with independent master/Colorize parameter storage, 0–100 Colorize saturation and four wrapped range/falloff bounds. Ported the original HSL conversions, 4096-sample additive hue-range tables and near-gray range weighting. Identity, excluded colors and hidden RGB retain exact samples. Native float samples are decoded to linear light before the source transfer and encoded back; extended results remain finite or fail transactionally on overflow. Exact Photoshop HSL/HDR behavior remains unqualified.

Connected Image → Adjustments → Hue/Saturation (⌘U), editable adjustment layers, the Adjustments panel, Properties and a preview/cancel sheet. The controls include Master/six-color selection, before/after swatches, H/S/L sliders, Colorize, a draggable range/falloff wheel, numeric degrees, range/all resets and AHU preset load/save. The current selection becomes a new layer’s mask; slider/wheel edits coalesce for undo. Desaturate (⇧⌘U) applies the HSL saturation=-100 operation to selected pixels on the active raster layer as one background transaction. Existing legacy saturation layers remain unchanged.

Implemented strict 100-byte version-2 hue2/AHU import/export, preserving both active and inactive settings and unchanged source padding. Invalid values/order/version/length reject; imports retain masks, clipping and groups at RGB/8/16/32, and edits or rasterization replace/remove stale tags. Eight native mathematical preset definitions match the installed Photoshop 2026 files byte for byte when encoded; no Adobe files are bundled. Independent Python packing supplies the binary oracle.

Fifteen tests cover core math/storage/ranges, imaging/PSD and app workflows. The unchanged Rust functions and match arm generate 8,940 RGB and 8,940 encoded-float references. Native behavior intentionally differs for Colorize saturation below 25 (zero must be achromatic) and five explicitly indexed no-effect HDR inputs where Rust changes the original samples. These are pinned separately rather than hidden by relaxed tolerances. References: HUE_SATURATION_ORACLE.json, PHOTOSHOP_HUE_SATURATION_PRESETS.json and artifacts/hue-saturation-tests.log.

Added deterministic offscreen native control rendering with --snapshot-adjustments. The first render exposed radial/vertical striping from individual hue strokes; native conic/linear gradients corrected it. The inspected 990×820 artifact contains all three adjustment editors at the default 298-pixel content width, with readable controls and no clipped fields. AppKit startup needed the existing elevated test environment; a stalled sandboxed helper was terminated. This render neither captures the desktop nor verifies live Photoshop interactions. The Mac remains locked. Prominent-color detection, contextual Adjust Colors, targeted image sampling and exact live behavior remain pending.

The full package suite passes 264 tests with zero failures/skips; the three existing StoreKit integration results remain historical and were not rerun. Universal release packaging and clean ZIP extraction verify both macOS 14 deployment targets, the ad-hoc signature, Photato branding, unchanged supplied icon and retained licenses. ## 2026-10-07 — RGB Curves editing and interchange

Added the native RGB/channel Curves model and ported the source's natural cubic spline/4096-sample transfer. Each component is applied before the composite curve. Validation permits 2–19 distinct increasing input coordinates and bounded 0…255 outputs; reversed and nonmonotonic output curves are supported. Identity edits, untouched channels, alpha and hidden colors retain their stored samples. Destructive edits respect transformed layer selection coverage, apply in the background and commit atomically with one undo. Float storage is decoded into linear light for the bounded source curve and partial-mask mixing; HDR extrapolation and exact Photoshop behavior remain unqualified.

The Properties editor and native ⌘M sheet expose a channel picker, histogram, grid, draggable/addable points, numeric coordinates, arrow/Shift-arrow movement, Delete, point selection, reset and preview/cancel. Input analysis shares the Levels worker, so changing the target curve does not invalidate the histogram. New Curves layers inherit the selection mask. The shared draft guard also prevents clipboard mutations and Free Transform during Levels/Curves previews. Remaining Curves interactions include freehand mode, eyedroppers, Auto, targeted image sampling, multi-point selection, full display options and live verification.

Implemented strict PSD point-mode curv and ACV version-1 bitmap/version-4 count codecs, with bounded extension records and byte preservation for unchanged source data. Unsupported freehand, additional active channels, conflicting records and incompatible profile/metadata semantics reject. Edits replace stale tags; rasterization and content changes remove them. PSD hierarchy, clipping and masks remain editable at 8/16/32 bits. Nine installed Photoshop preset definitions match the native mathematical settings; Adobe preset files are not bundled.

Four core, six imaging and four app tests cover independent Rust spline/combined tables, malformed and truncated binary fixtures, installed presets, PSD/ImageIO decode, alpha/masks/groups, native storage, point limits, locks, preview/cancel/background commit/undo, coalescing, bounded preset IO and ⌘M menu registration. An initial float test exposed encode/decode rounding amplified by steep curves: the final test compares the independent Rust table at the actual linear input and separately bounds the encoding round-trip. No production tolerance or Photoshop acceptance requirement was relaxed. References: CURVES_ORACLE.json, PHOTOSHOP_CURVES_PRESETS.json, artifacts/curves-tests.log. These establish the current port's tested behavior, not Photoshop byte equivalence.

The full package suite passes 249 tests with zero failures/skips; the three existing StoreKit integration results remain historical and were not rerun. Both release architectures target macOS 14, and clean ZIP extraction verifies the ad-hoc signature, unchanged supplied icon, bundle metadata and license integrity. These full-image CPU operations remain too slow for immediate slider feedback at this size; tiled/incremental rendering is still needed. ## 2026-10-07 — Levels histograms, presets and automatic correction

Added full-resolution RGB/channel histograms to the native Levels editor. A renderer probe captures the actual input before the target adjustment, including prior adjustments, Pass Through/isolated groups and clipping chains. Own parameters, masks and upper layers do not contaminate the input histogram. Destructive Levels analyzes source pixels inside the document/selection, honoring layer coordinates and selection holes. Histogram calculation runs in a cancellable background task; identical input is retained during slider changes and stale completions cannot replace newer results. Float documents are analyzed in the linear sample domain used by their Levels evaluator. Visible pixels count once; fractional selection weighting and Photoshop histogram display exactness remain unqualified.

Read the installed Photoshop 2026 Levels presets and discovered that their version-3 ALV extension contains 62 records. The prior parser incorrectly borrowed the PSD channel-count limit of 56. The bounded reader now accepts up to 256 records and still rejects meaningful unrepresented extra channels. Eight native mathematical preset definitions match the installed records; unchanged ALV source bytes round-trip exactly. The application bundles native settings, not Adobe's files. Load/Save Levels Preset uses native file panels, bounded reads and security-scoped access. Evidence and source hashes: PHOTOSHOP_LEVELS_PRESETS.json.

Ported the source's automatic histogram clipping and median-neutralization estimator. Image → Auto Tone, Auto Contrast and Auto Color use ⇧⌘L, ⌥⇧⌘L and ⇧⌘B, honor selection/locks, run in the background and undo as a single named transaction. The Levels Auto menu offers the same three choices with independent shadow/highlight clipping percentages (default 0.10%). Pixel commands keep estimator precision; editable controls round gamma to hundredths. This is not Photoshop's full Auto Options dialog: target colors, Snap Neutral Midtones, Enhance Brightness and Contrast and saved defaults remain pending. Auto Color is the Rust approximation, not a claim of Photoshop's dark/light-color algorithm.

Eleven additional tests cover fifteen independent Rust histogram/auto cases, clipping/invalid/transparent samples, depth/selection/coordinates, transactional commands, grouped/clipped adjustment inputs, 62-record preset extensions and truncations, all eight installed presets, bounded native preset file reads, cache/cancellation/stale results and app command wiring. The full suite passes 235 tests with zero failures/skips on this Mac. The installed-preset reference test explicitly skips when Photoshop 2026 is absent; portable binary tests remain mandatory. The existing three StoreKit integration tests were not rerun because purchase code is unchanged.

References: [Adobe automatic corrections](https://helpx.adobe.com/photoshop/desktop/adjust-color/color-corrections/apply-auto-tone-auto-contrast-and-auto-color.html), [Adobe Levels and presets](https://helpx.adobe.com/photoshop/using/levels-adjustment.html), and the read-only installed preset files. The Mac remains locked, so new live control/file-panel interactions and Photoshop rendering comparisons are not verified.

Added editable RGB/composite, Red, Green and Blue Levels, input black/white, gamma, output black/white and Reset. The original Rust levels_q, levels_float and LUT sampling functions generate 5,418 independent regression vectors. RGB/8 uses its whole-level input rounding; RGB/16 uses its 32768-step working scale. The integer compositor retains the source’s 4096-entry master-then-channel LUT and fitted shadow toe. This is source-math qualification within Float tolerance, not byte-identical Photoshop rendering. RGB/32 evaluates on linear-light samples and preserves extended values instead of using the source display LUT’s clipped interval; fractional selection/mask/opacity mixing also uses linear light. Identity edits preserve bits and alpha. Overflow fails transactionally rather than turning non-finite results into black.

PSD interchange now supports represented RGB levl records, empty adjustment caches, pixel/vector masks, opacity and clipping metadata. Version-2 records and the optional Lvls extension are bounded and validated; meaningful unrepresented extra channels reject. A Python-packed binary fixture is independent of the Swift writer, all truncations fail, unchanged source blocks survive, and parameter edits replace stale blocks. Native-created RGB/8/16/32 files also expose readable merged images through ImageIO. Non-sRGB adjustment profiles, additional CgEd data and unsupported parameter precision remain explicit limitations.

Twelve new tests cover the oracle, endpoints/hidden color/HDR, alpha, selection, native persistence, PSD records and corruption, mask/opacity composition, layer creation/edit/undo, preview/cancel/commit/cancelled work, locks and menu/shortcut wiring. All 224 package tests pass; prior three StoreKit integration tests qualify unchanged purchase code. Photoshop and live UI qualification remain unavailable while the Mac is locked. Histograms, Auto/Auto Options, eyedroppers, presets and full group/clip interaction qualification remain open.

## 2026-10-07 — compact raster archives and faster pixel packing

Profiled dense native serialization and clipboard format preparation. A 1,048,576-pixel RGB/16 gradient/alpha document previously spent 1,398.61 ms encoding and 1,950.92 ms decoding its 33,841,297-byte property-list archive. Surface now encodes portable little-endian Float32 RGBA tiles, with lossless macOS zlib compression where smaller. Native archive version 7 retains previous model semantics and reads versions 1–6. A checked-in file produced by the unchanged version-6 release verifies actual migration. Older app builds cannot read new version-7 files. Both encode and decode enforce the 512 MiB file cap; a shared decode budget reserves each layer/mask's tile storage before expansion, including legacy tiles.

Binary sparse-default samples also preserve Float bits. Tests caught property-list numeric deduplication changing the sign of zero in a default pixel; default samples now use explicit bytes, as the tiles do. Seven new tests cover an independent Python/zlib oracle, raw little-endian samples, signed zero/subnormals/hidden and extended RGB, real legacy migration, all output depths and tile edges, malformed/truncated/trailing streams, invalid indices/versions and aggregate raster budgets. All 212 package tests pass with zero failures/skips; unchanged StoreKit integration retains its prior three passing tests.

CoreGraphics display/float/TIFF buffers now pack directly from tiles without per-pixel array creation or dictionary lookup. A single unmodified pixel-layer clipboard preview bypasses unnecessary composition/depth conversion. The same sparse constant-color 24 MP RGB/16 clipboard sample prepares native/TIFF/PNG in 203.81 ms, down from 6,729.88 ms, with identical native samples and unchanged external TIFF/PNG sizes. This is about a 33× improvement for that sample; pasteboard IPC, merged/selection copies and general workload percentiles are not included.

The dense one-megapixel archive now saves/loads in 94.05/21.07 ms and occupies 5,834,421 bytes. A dense 24 MP sample saves/loads in 2,070.68/488.37 ms, occupies 130,037,175 bytes and preserves native samples. Its image packing/encoding checks also succeed. The benchmark retains both documents and all outputs concurrently and reaches 2,423,128,064 bytes of process residency; this is not a per-operation memory budget or full large-document performance qualification. Exact measurements: `artifacts/performance-packing-before.json`, `performance-packing-after.json`, `performance-packing-24mp.json` and `performance-clipboard-24mp.json`.

Universal arm64/x86_64 release, both macOS 14 deployment targets, original icon/license integrity and clean ZIP ad-hoc signature pass. Existing Photoshop PSD fidelity differences remain unchanged. No fresh live UI/Photoshop comparison was performed during this headless storage/performance checkpoint; previous live checks remain historical evidence. Full Photoshop functionality, preferences, shortcuts and byte equivalence remain open.

## 2026-10-07 — native image and layer clipboard

Implemented Cut, Copy, Copy Merged, Paste, Paste in Place, Paste Into/Outside and New from Clipboard. A pixel selection lifts coverage from the active content; no-selection Copy preserves selected editable layers and group subtrees, including off-canvas pixels, masks, clipping, opacity and type layout. Native clipboard data retains source coordinates and full sample precision. Paste remaps layer IDs, converts to destination depth, inserts above the active layer or into its group, and uses a single undo transaction. Paste Into/Outside converts destination coverage into an unlinked document-space mask. Standard AppKit responder routing keeps text-field clipboard behavior intact; image menu shortcuts match the documented Photoshop commands.

Clipboard preparation runs off the main thread. Cut writes the prepared clipboard before committing the validated edit; cancellation, write failures and stale document/clipboard state leave the document intact. Native clipboard archives are bounded and versioned. TIFF retains 8/16/32-bit components; PNG provides an SDR fallback. Tests use an isolated named macOS pasteboard and never modify the general clipboard. The coding-tool sandbox initially denied pasteboard service access; the approved native test run passes with that service available.

Sixteen new tests cover core/model/render/app behavior: fractional selection and transparency bounds, hidden and extended RGB, native archive damage, depth conversion, group remapping, masks, editable type layout, merged visible content, external TIFF/PNG precision/orientation, menu wiring, cancellation and failed Cut. Aligned pixels initially passed through interpolation, changing Float bits; direct sample reads corrected that. The first TIFF implementation promoted integer content to float, which was corrected to retain source precision. All 205 package tests now pass with zero failures/skips. Existing three StoreKit integration tests cover unchanged purchase code and were not rerun.

A single constant-color 24 MP RGB/16 release sample measured layer capture at 0.34 ms, preparation of all native/TIFF/PNG representations at 6,729.88 ms, and native decode plus paste at 0.40 ms. Native pixels remain exact. The corrected TIFF is 192,019,366 bytes versus 384,035,366 before the depth correction; this is a size/precision correction, not a measured speed improvement. This sparse-layer sample excludes pasteboard IPC and does not qualify dense/merged/selection copy latency, peak memory or p95 performance. Full image encoding remains a significant cost despite running in the background.

## 2026-10-07 — compressed and high-depth PSD interchange

Added bounded raw/RLE/ZIP/ZIP-prediction channels at 8/16/32 bits, depth-specific Lr16/Lr32 sections, neutral HDR conversion metadata, high-precision raster masks, flattened RGB input, and compressed pixel-layer export. Swift implements PSD layout/prediction/sample handling; DEFLATE uses the macOS system zlib. No Rust runtime or third-party package is linked. Saved alpha/spot channels and unrepresented rich layer semantics still reject explicitly.

Live Photoshop 2026 opened both high-depth exports with editable layers/masks. The first float export revealed a gamma mismatch that round-trip tests alone missed: Photoshop uses linear 32-bit samples. Corrected PSD transfer/profile handling and float layer composition, preserving extended and hidden RGB values. Native working samples remain sRGB-encoded. Older float development documents may change appearance at layer overlaps as a consequence of the corrected composition. Full HDR blend/adjustment/display fidelity remains open.

110 tests pass. Nine new tests cover 12 independent Python/zlib compression vectors, malformed ZIP streams, all depth/export-compression combinations, flattened inputs, HDR/negative/hidden samples, linear compositing, Apple high-depth PSD decoding and two new Photoshop-saved references. Those reference layers/masks agree within 0.00005 and recomposed channels within 0.0001. The original Photoshop Untitled-1 was left untouched. Groups, clipping, vectors, PSB and the full source corpus remain next.

Recorded the user’s intent to release the native Swift work as an upstream fork with voluntary donations. Donation and fork URLs remain unset pending the maintainer’s choices, so no payment recipient is invented. This does not replace the active Photoshop-parity objective.

101 tests pass, including absent/valid/invalid release destinations. Both architectures build; the clean ZIP signature, license copies and unset destination metadata verify after extraction. Live UI checks confirm Help → Open Source & Credits, the native About view, the bundled license sheet and hidden support controls without a configured recipient.

<a id="dev-step-007"></a>

## 2026-10-07 — independent layer masks and PSD interoperability

Added native mask transforms, outside coverage, enabled/link states, density and Gaussian feather (sigma equals mask radius, independent of selection feather). Brushes grow tightly cropped mask rectangles, honor selection bounds and preserve existing coordinates. Unlinked mask Move leaves layer pixels fixed; layer transforms preserve unlinked masks in document space, while whole-canvas transforms include them. Properties now switches to mask controls when the mask thumbnail is selected. Undo and native archive v4 retain all mask properties; v1–v3 remain readable.

Extended RGB/8 PSD raw/RLE import and raw export to raster masks, including real-mask metadata and both observed parameter orderings. Fixed a pre-existing reversal of Photoshop layer-record order. Preserve selected layer tags in the native document/export and accept ordinary ICC-managed Photoshop 2026 metadata while rejecting actual OCIO workflows. Rich vectors/groups/type/effects and unsupported compression/depth still reject explicitly. Affine mask PSD export remains pending; native storage is preserved.

Fetched seven MIT psd-tools files at a pinned commit with verified hashes. Their rich layers are not a full import pass. Created two separate fixtures through the actual Photoshop 2026 UI; the raster derivative and re-saved density/feather export are bundled with provenance and license. Photoshop visibly read 50% density and 1.5 px feather, and both stored Photoshop composites agree with the Swift renderer within the asserted 2/255 channel tolerance. Original user Photoshop document and public corpus files unchanged. 99 tests pass, including selection regressions, mask expansion and independent mask moves. Full Photoshop parity remains the active goal.

<a id="dev-step-006"></a>

## 2026-10-07 — coverage selections and direct mask editing

Ported PhotoCraft's GRAY8 selection combination and even-odd polygon scan conversion semantics to native Swift. Added ellipse/freehand/polygon tools, modifier and options-bar combinations, inverse, Gaussian feathering, and true coverage clipping for painting/fill/clear/adjustment/blur/mask creation. Canvas geometry resamples selection coverage. Native archive v3 stores coverage compactly and reads v1/v2. Added separate mask/content thumbnail targets, grayscale mask brush/fill/invert and transformed-mask stroke coordinates without rasterizing live type/shapes. Cancellable background selection commands reuse transactional editing.

87 tests pass. New tests cover source polygon/feather cases, fractional combinations, all pixel depths, geometry/undo/archive preservation, mask alpha independence and transformed coordinates, locked masks, freehand gesture completion, polygon closure/cancellation and target isolation. Clean builds resolved stale SwiftPM test objects after Core enum/layout changes; test targets now explicitly declare directly imported Core/Imaging dependencies. Live UI verified ellipse subtraction and ring Fill, coverage-to-mask, mask painting, thumbnail target switching, polygon Return closure and 20 px feathered Fill; all test edits were undone. Inspected Photoshop's selection flyout/Lasso options without editing its document. Advanced selection refinement, Quick Mask, density/feather/apply/disable mask controls and PSD mask fidelity remain open.

## 2026-10-07 — native geometry module

Added affine live type/shape geometry, raster/mask resampling, Free Transform handles and numeric options, image/canvas size sheets, resolution-only sizing, five whole-canvas turns, and one-step undo. Heavy operations are cancellable background transactions with stale-result protection. Native archive v2 reads v1 and preserves matrices/layout/resolution; ImageIO and the limited PSD codec preserve print resolution. Live inspection of Photoshop's size dialogs guided UI behavior. Fixed the physical-unit feedback loop found in native live checks. All 71 tests pass; both architectures build and the clean ZIP signature verifies. Dense 24 MP rotation measured 2825 ms and bicubic 24→6 MP resize 1445 ms in one release sample. Advanced transforms and selection/mask workflows remain open.

Inspected source architecture and honest parity assessment; inventoried 24 crates and 626 unique source menu IDs. ### Initial implementation sequence — reconstructed on 2026-10-08

This expansion uses the original milestone narrative, the frozen source inventory, current porting plan and surviving early logs. It records the known work sequence; it does not recreate missing edit-by-edit history or assert exact times for undocumented actions.

1. **Source discovery and requirements.** Inspected the Rust architecture and its own parity limits; inventoried 24 crates / 257,925 source lines and 626 unique menu IDs. Preserved source hashes in `docs/SOURCE_INVENTORY.json`, created the command matrix and planned a native macOS 14+ port. The source is an extracted snapshot; no upstream base commit is available.
2. **Native architecture.** Created `Package.swift` and separate Core, Imaging, AI and application targets. Kept the original Rust tree and upstream notices. The plan defines value-based document/commands, native AppKit/SwiftUI interaction, independent optional AI and a universal deployment target.
3. **Core implementation and differential evidence.** Added sparse raster storage, pixel depths, blend math, document/layers, editing commands and transactional history. `docs/BLEND_ORACLE.json` records 972 executed Rust blend vectors and the generating source hash.
4. **Imaging and format integration.** Added native composition, raster I/O, text/shapes, blur/resize, the first strict PSD subset and native document persistence. The early imaging run at **2026-10-07 14:33:14 (local log time; zone not embedded)** reports seven tests and one Gaussian-blur failure. A later integration run at 14:38:48 reports 24 tests with no failures; the 14:39:35 module run reports 25 with no failures. The surviving logs establish those outcomes, not every intervening source edit.
5. **Native workspace and document lifecycle.** Connected the canvas, tools, document controller, save/open/export and undo. The 15:06:11 workflow run reports 29 passing tests. The original milestone records live editing/save, full/minimum layouts, a 24 MP CPU baseline and repairs for redraw, preview tone mapping, imported-file protection and gesture history.
6. **Optional AI and platform gating.** Added the initial local prompt/image adapters and generated-layer undo. The 15:08:08 AI-integration run reports 29 passing tests. Later entries separately document the corrected macOS 27 cloud policy and the user's subsequent Private Cloud Compute authorization; do not apply today's policy retroactively to the initial build.
7. **Packaging and qualification.** Built both architectures with macOS 14 deployment settings and weak-linked optional frameworks. The early core log records a code-signing build failure with no test summary. The milestone records subsequent packaging/signature repairs and a 40-test pass, but this audit does not recover an immutable full 40-test log. That final count remains a historical narrative claim rather than a newly verified run.

Surviving early evidence: [core build failure](../artifacts/core-tests.log), [imaging failure](../artifacts/imaging-tests.log), [24-test integration](../artifacts/integration-build.log), [25-test modules](../artifacts/module-tests.log), [workflow tests](../artifacts/workflow-tests.log), [AI integration](../artifacts/ai-integration-tests.log), [source inventory](SOURCE_INVENTORY.json), and [blend provenance](BLEND_ORACLE.json). No private user documents or proprietary implementation code were added by this reconstruction.
